# DLC Circuit Troubleshooting (L15B7/L15BA/L15BY: With CAN gateway) (2020 2021)

NOTE: If you suspect the HDS or the HDS DLC cable may be the source of the communication problem, verify that they are working properly by connecting them to a known-good, similar vehicle and system, then check for communication problems.

- HDS communication check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Connect the HDS to the DLC. NOTE: Make sure the HDS is properly connected to the DLC. -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, and turn on the HDS. Does the HDS identify the vehicle? YES Go to step 2. NO Go to step 17.

-2. Connect the HDS to the DLC.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, and turn on the HDS.

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

Go to step 17.

L-LINE

-4. Check for continuity between DLC (16P) No. 7 and these connector terminals (K LINE line), or DLC (16P) No. 15 and these connector terminals (L-LINE line):

K LINE

Connector | Terminal

Immobilizer-keyless control unit 7P connector (without keyless access system) | No. 3

Audio unit connector A (24P) (without navigation) | No. 12

Audio-navigation unit connector A (24P) (with navigation) | No. 12

Body control module connector D (40P) | No. 36

Active sound control unit 20P connector (with active sound control unit) | No. 14

L-LINE

Connector | Terminal

Body control module connector F (36P) (with keyless access system) | No. 21

SRS unit connector A (39P) | No. 33

Is there continuity between the DLC and the terminals in the chart?

YES

Go to step 15.

NO

Repair an open in the wire between the DLC (K LINE wire or L-LINE wire) and the appropriate connector.

# DLC Circuit Troubleshooting (L15B7/L15BA: With CAN gateway) (2016 2017 2018 2019)

NOTE: If you suspect the HDS or the HDS DLC cable may be the source of the communication problem, verify that they are working properly by connecting them to a known-good, similar vehicle and system, and checking for communication problems.

- HDS communication check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Connect the HDS to the DLC. NOTE: Make sure the HDS is properly connected to the DLC. -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, and turn on the HDS. Does the HDS identify the vehicle? YES Go to step 2. NO Go to step 17.

-2. Connect the HDS to the DLC.

Does the HDS identify the vehicle?

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

Go to step 17.

NO If the HDS does not communicate with the SRS, go to step 5. NO If the HDS does not communicate with the VSA system, go to step 6. NO If the HDS does not communicate with the DRIVING SUPPORT system, go to step 13.

CAN gateway 12P connector PCM connector A (50P) -4. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Is there continuity? YES The F-CAN A_H wire is OK. Go to step 4. NO Repair an open in the F-CAN A_H wire between the PCM (A37) and the CAN gateway.

# DLC Circuit Troubleshooting (L15B7/L15BA: Without CAN gateway)

NOTE: If you suspect the HDS or the HDS DLC cable may be the source of the communication problem, verify that they are working properly by connecting them to a known-good, similar vehicle and system, and checking for communication problems.

- HDS communication check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Connect the HDS to the DLC. NOTE: Make sure the HDS is properly connected to the DLC. -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, and turn on the HDS. Does the HDS identify the vehicle? YES Go to step 2. NO Go to step 14.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Connect the HDS to the DLC.

NOTE: Make sure the HDS is properly connected to the DLC.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, and turn on the HDS.

Does the HDS identify the vehicle?

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

Go to step 14.

- Determine possible failure area (PGM-FI system, other systems) -1. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs in the PGM-FI system with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC Are any Pending or Confirmed DTCs indicated? YES Go to the indicated DTC's troubleshooting. NO If the HDS does not communicate with the SRS, go to step 3. NO If the HDS does not communicate with the VSA system, go to step 4. NO If the HDS does not communicate with the EPS system, go to step 5. NO If the HDS does not communicate with the IMMOBI (immobilizer) system, go to step 6. NO If the HDS does not communicate with the ONE-PUSH START system, go to step 7. NO If the HDS does not communicate with the TPMS, go to step 8. NO If the HDS does not communicate with the AT system, go to step 9. NO If the HDS does not communicate with the BODY ELECTRICAL system, go to step 10.

YES

NO

If the HDS does not communicate with the SRS, go to step 3.

NO

NO

NO

- SRS check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, and watch the SRS indicator. Does the SRS indicator come on and go off? YES The SRS is OK. Go to step 11. NO Go to the symptom troubleshooting for SRS indicator stays on, but no DTCs are stored, or cannot be read .

Go to step 11. NO Go to the symptom troubleshooting for ABS indicator, brake system indicator (red), VSA indicator, and brake system indicator (amber) do not go off .

# DLC Circuit Troubleshooting (L15B7/L15BY: With CAN gateway) (2019)

NOTE: If you suspect the HDS or the HDS DLC cable may be the source of the communication problem, verify that they are working properly by connecting them to a known-good, similar vehicle and system, then check for communication problems.

- HDS communication check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Connect the HDS to the DLC. NOTE: Make sure the HDS is properly connected to the DLC. -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, and turn on the HDS. Does the HDS identify the vehicle? YES Go to step 2. NO Go to step 16.

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

# DLC Circuit Troubleshooting(Si) (2017 2018 2019)

NOTE: If you suspect the HDS or the HDS DLC cable may be the source of the communication problem, verify that they are working properly by connecting them to a known-good, similar vehicle and system, and checking for communication problems.

- HDS communication check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Connect the HDS to the DLC. NOTE: Make sure the HDS is properly connected to the DLC. -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, and turn on the HDS. Does the HDS identify the vehicle? YES Go to step 2. NO Go to step 12.

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

SRS unit connector A (39P) -4. Do the 12 volt battery terminal reconnection procedure . -5. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, and read the HDS. Does the HDS identify the vehicle? YES Replace the SRS unit . NO Substitute a known-good body control module , then recheck. If the HDS identifies the vehicle and the body control module was substituted, replace the original body control module . ## Chunk 2169: F-CAN Circuit Troubleshooting (K20C2: Without CAN gateway)

- Title: F-CAN Circuit Troubleshooting (K20C2: Without CAN gateway)

### Full Text

````text
e vehicle and the PCM was substituted, replace the original PCM .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Do the 12 volt battery terminal disconnection procedure , and wait for at least 3 minutes.

-3. Disconnect the following connector.

SRS unit connector A (39P)

-4. Do the 12 volt battery terminal reconnection procedure .

-5. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, and read the HDS.

Does the HDS identify the vehicle?

YES

Replace the SRS unit .

NO

- Substitute a known-good body control module , then recheck. If the HDS identifies the vehicle and the body control module was substituted, replace the original body control module .

- Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting, or substitute a known-good PCM , then recheck. If the HDS identifies the vehicle and the PCM was substituted, replace the original PCM .
````

YES

Replace the SRS unit .

NO

- Substitute a known-good body control module , then recheck. If the HDS identifies the vehicle and the body control module was substituted, replace the original body control module .

## Chunk 2189: Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode 1 and Test Mode 2 (without the HDS) (2/4-door)

- Title: Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode 1 and Test Mode 2 (without the HDS) (2/4-door)
- Source path: `pages\1061.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_6155f5ebc981`
- Images: `images\GHH410981.jpeg`, `images\GHH410982.jpeg`, `images\GHH410983.jpeg`, `images\GHH410984.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\12962.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode 1 and Test Mode 2 (without the HDS) (2/4-door)

Shift to Test Mode 1

Check the PCM for DTCs and troubleshoot PCM or F-CAN loss of communication errors first, then do this diagnosis if the HDS is not available.

1. Check the No. A18 (10 A) fuse in the under-hood fuse/relay box and the No. B7 (10 A) fuse in the under-dash fuse/relay box. If a fuse is blown, find and correct the cause, then replace the fuse.

2. Move the front individual map light switch OFF, the interior light switch to the DOOR position, the ceiling light switch to the MIDDLE position, and close all doors.

NOTE: Do steps 3-7 within 10 seconds.

3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

4. Pull and hold the passing switch in its PASSING position.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

5. While holding the combination light switch is in PASSING position, turn to the PARKING position, then release, pull, and hold the combination light switch in its PASSING position.

6. Turn the combination light switch to OFF, then turn to the PARKING position, and turn to OFF two more times.

7. Release the combination light switch.

8. Wait 5 seconds, and watch the front individual map lights and ceiling light. When the lights flash quickly once, the system enters in Test Mode 1.

9. If the individual map lights keeps blinking after entering Test Mode 1. Check for B-CAN unit communication error.

Error | Display code(blinking pattern)

Communication bus line error (BUS OFF). | 1

The body control module cannot receive signals from the gauge control module. | 5

10. Check for B-CAN DTCs indicated by the LCD display while still in Test Mode 1. Press the TRIP button to display the next code. After you get to the last code, the display shows END. If no DTCs are stored, the display reads NO.

NOTE: If the test times out, turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, and repeat steps 3

Without Multi-Information Display (MID)

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

With Multi-Information Display (MID)

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The control unit that has stored the code can be identified by the number shown after the letter "PA" on the multi-information display (MID).

Control Unit | Control Unit I.D. Number

Body control module | 18

Power window master switch | 30

Gauge control module | 50

Climate control unit | 51

Automatic lighting control unit-sensor | 72

Automatic lighting/rain sensor | 74

Immobilizer-keyless control unit | 96

Front seat heater control unit | A2

Wireless charger | AF

NOTE: You can also check B-CAN communication status by the following methods:

- The body control module self-diagnostic function Test Mode 1 and Test Mode 2 can check some communication statuses and some switch circuits.

- The gauge control module self-diagnostic function can check communication status .

Record all DTCs and sort them by DTC type, then troubleshoot them in this order:

- Internal error DTCs

- Loss of communication DTCs

- Signal error DTCs

12. Clear the DTCs by pressing and holding the SEL/RESET knob for about 10 seconds.

13. You will hear a beep to confirm the codes have been cleared. Operate the devices that failed, and recheck for codes.

Shift to Test Mode 2

NOTE: Do steps 14-17 within 10 seconds.

14. Pull and hold the combination light switch in its PASSING position.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

15. While holding the combination light switch is in PASSING position, turn to the PARKING position, then release, pull, and hold the combination light switch in its PASSING position.

16. Turn the combination light switch to OFF, then turn to the PARKING position, and turn to OFF two more times.

17. Release the combination light switch.

18. Wait 5 seconds, and watch the individual map lights. When the lights flash two times quickly and then go off, the system is in Test Mode 2.

NOTE: If you repeat this procedure, the system automatically returns to Test Mode 1.

19. The following tables list the circuits that can be checked in Test Mode 2. Operate the switch that is most closely related to the problem. If the circuit is OK, the individual map lights, and ceiling light blink once. If the circuit is faulty, there is no such indication.

- - If the front individual map lights and ceiling light do not blink even when a switch is operated, check two or three other circuits listed in the table.
````

## Chunk 2191: Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode 1 and Test Mode 2 (without the HDS) (5-door) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

- Title: Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode 1 and Test Mode 2 (without the HDS) (5-door) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)
- Source path: `pages\1062.html`

````text
# Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode 1 and Test Mode 2 (without the HDS) (5-door) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

Shift to Test Mode 1

Check the PCM for DTCs and troubleshoot PCM or F-CAN loss of communication errors first, then do this diagnosis if the HDS is not available.

1. Check the No. A18 (10 A) fuse in the under-hood fuse/relay box, and the No. B7 (10 A) fuse in the under-dash fuse/relay box. If a fuse is blown, find and correct the cause, then replace the fuse.

2. Move the front individual map light switch OFF, the interior light switch to the DOOR position, the ceiling light switch to the MIDDLE position, and close all doors and tailgate.

NOTE:

3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

4. Pull and hold the passing switch in its PASSING position.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

5. While holding the combination light switch is in PASSING position, turn to the PARKING position, then release, pull, and hold the combination light switch in its PASSING position.

6. Turn the combination light switch to OFF, then turn to the PARKING position, and turn to OFF two more times.

7. Release the combination light switch.

8. Wait 5 seconds, and watch the front individual map lights and ceiling light. When the lights flash quickly once, the system enters in Test Mode 1.

9. If the individual map lights keeps blinking after entering Test Mode 1. Check for B-CAN unit communication error.

Error | Display code(blinking pattern)

Communication bus line error (BUS OFF). | 1

The body control module cannot receive signals from the gauge control module. | 5

10. Check for B-CAN DTCs indicated by the LCD display while still in Test Mode 1. Press the TRIP button to display the next code. After you get to the last code, the display shows END. If no DTCs are stored, the display reads NO.

NOTE: If the test times out, turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, and repeat steps 3

Without Multi-Information Display (MID)

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

With Multi-Information Display (MID)

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The control unit that has stored the code can be identified by the number shown after the letter "PA" on the multi-information display (MID).

Control Unit | Control Unit I.D. Number

Body control module | 18

Power window master switch | 30

Gauge control module | 50

Climate control unit | 51

Automatic lighting control unit-sensor | 72

Automatic lighting/rain sensor | 74

Immobilizer-keyless control unit | 96

Parking and back-up sensor control unit | 9A

Front seat heater control unit | A2

Wireless charger | AF

NOTE: You can also check B-CAN communication status by the following methods:

- The body control module self-diagnostic function Test Mode 1 and Test Mode 2 can check some communication statuses and some switch circuits.

- The gauge control module self-diagnostic function can check communication status .

Record all DTCs and sort them by DTC type, then troubleshoot them in this order:

- Internal error DTCs

- Loss of communication DTCs

- Signal error DTCs

12. Clear the DTCs by pressing and holding the SEL/RESET knob for about 10 seconds.

13. You will hear a beep to confirm the codes have been cleared. Operate the devices that failed, and recheck for codes.

Shift to Test Mode 2

NOTE: Do steps 14-17 within 10 seconds.

14. Pull and hold the combination light switch in its PASSING position.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

15. While holding the combination light switch is in PASSING position, turn to the PARKING position, then release, pull, and hold the combination light switch in its PASSING position.

16. Turn the combination light switch to OFF, then turn to the PARKING position, and turn to OFF two more times.

17. Release the combination light switch.

18. Wait 5 seconds, and watch the individual map lights. When the lights flash two times quickly and then go off, the system is in Test Mode 2.

NOTE: If you repeat this procedure, the system automatically returns to Test Mode 1.

19. The following tables list the circuits that can be checked in Test Mode 2. Operate the switch that is most closely related to the problem. If the circuit is OK, the individual map lights and ceiling light blink once. If the circuit is faulty, there is no such indication.

- - If the front individual map lights and ceiling light do not blink even when a switch is operated, check two or three other circuits listed in the table.
````

# Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A - Initial Communication and DTC Checks

Check the PCM for DTCs and troubleshoot PCM or F-CAN loss of communication errors first, then do this diagnosis if the symptom is related to the B-CAN system.

Compare the symptom with this list of B-CAN related systems:

- Gauge control module

- Exterior lights

- Turn signals

- Entry light control

- Interior lights

- Safety indicators

- Horns (security and panic)

- Reminder chimes (seat belt, lights-on, and parking brake)

- Power window/moonroof timer

- Wiper/washer

- Security

- Keyless entry

- Key interlock

- Power door locks

- Dashlights brightness

- Immobilizer

- Daytime running lights

- Keyless access control

- Climate control

- Seat heater

- Wireless charger

- - If the symptom is related to the B-CAN system, go to step 2.

If the symptom is related to the B-CAN system, go to step 2.

- - If the symptom is not related to the B-CAN system, go to the system symptom troubleshooting.

If the symptom is not related to the B-CAN system, go to the system symptom troubleshooting.

2. Connect the HDS to the data link connector (DLC) (A), then turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

From the BODY ELECTRICAL SYSTEM SELECT menu, select B-CAN CONTROL UNITS INFORMATION, and then select CHECK CONNECTED CONTROL UNITS listed to see if the following control units are communicating with the HDS.

- MICU

- Gauge control module

- Climate control unit

- Automatic lighting/rain sensor

- Automatic lighting control unit-sensor

- Immobilizer-keyless control unit

- Door multiplex control unit

- Front seat heater control unit

NOTE:

- If a unit is communicating with the HDS, "DETECT" is displayed.

- If a unit is not communicating or the vehicle is not equipped, "NOT AVAILABLE" is displayed.

- The HDS only checks the status one time when BODY ELECTRICAL is selected. To recheck the status after repair, reboot the HDS.

- - If all control units are communicating with the HDS, go to step 4.

If all control units are communicating with the HDS, go to step 4.

- - If any of the control units are not communicating, go to B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode B .

If any of the control units are not communicating, go to B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode B .

- - If all units are not communicating or only the body control module is communicating, go to body control module DTC U1280 troubleshooting .

If all units are not communicating or only the body control module is communicating, go to body control module DTC U1280 troubleshooting .

Select the system that has the problem from the BODY ELECTRICAL SYSTEM SELECT menu, then select DTCs.

- - If any DTCs are indicated, go to step 5.

If any DTCs are indicated, go to step 5.

- - If the problem is related to one of the following items and the system that is malfunctioning does not stop or turn off, go to B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode C .

If the problem is related to one of the following items and the system that is malfunctioning does not stop or turn off, go to B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode C .

- - If the problem is related to one of the following items and the system that is malfunctioning does not run or turn on, go to B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode D .

If the problem is related to one of the following items and the system that is malfunctioning does not run or turn on, go to B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode D .

- Exterior lights Turn signals Entry light control Interior lights Horns (security and panic) Wiper/washer Power window Power door locks

- Exterior lights

- Turn signals

- Entry light control

- Interior lights

- Horns (security and panic)

- Wiper/washer

- Power window

- Power door locks

- - If the problem is related to one of the following items, go to the troubleshooting for that individual system.

If the problem is related to one of the following items, go to the troubleshooting for that individual system.

- Gauge control module Safety indicators Reminder chimes (seat belt, lights-on, and parking brake) Security Keyless entry Dashlights brightness Immobilizer Rear window defogger (climate control) Climate control Keyless access control Seat heater

- Gauge control module

- Safety indicators

- Reminder chimes (seat belt, lights-on, and parking brake)

- Security

- Climate control

# Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode B - Control Unit Not Communicating

Do this diagnosis if any of the control units are not communicating (NOT AVAILABLE is displayed in the HDS) as found by the B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

1. Using the HDS, select the system that has the symptom from the BODY ELECTRICAL SYSTEM SELECT menu.

2. Select DTCs, and then check for loss of communication DTCs.

- - If any loss of communication DTCs are indicated, go to step 3.

If any loss of communication DTCs are indicated, go to step 3.

- - If any loss of communication DTCs are not indicated, replace the body control module .

If any loss of communication DTCs are not indicated, replace the body control module .

3. Do the power, ground, and communication part of the input test for the unit(s) not communicating with the HDS.

Unit not communicating

Body control module

Gauge control module

Climate control unit

Power window master switch

Keyless access control unit

Immobilizer-keyless control unit

Automatic lighting/rain sensor

Automatic lighting control unit-sensor

Front seat heater control unit

Wireless charger

## Chunk 2196: Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode C - No DTCs and Output Won't Shut OFF

- Title: Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode C - No DTCs and Output Won't Shut OFF

### Full Text

````text
# Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode C - No DTCs and Output Won't Shut OFF

Do this diagnosis if a component that is controlled by the B-CAN system does not stop or turn off.

NOTE:

- If the component does not turn on, go to B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode D .

- See the B-CAN system unit input/output index for a list of input and output devices and the control units that monitor the input and control the output devices.

1. Check for DTCs by selecting the system MODE MENU from the HDS.

- - If any DTCs are indicated, go to B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

If any DTCs are indicated, go to B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

- - If any DTCs are not indicated, go to step 2.

If any DTCs are not indicated, go to step 2.

2. Turn off the switch that controls the malfunctioning component.

3. Select the applicable system from the system MODE MENU, then select DATA LIST, and check the input of the switch that controls the related component.

- - If HDS indicates the switch is OFF, go to step 4.

If HDS indicates the switch is OFF, go to step 4.

- - If HDS indicates the switch is not OFF, go to step 6.

If HDS indicates the switch is not OFF, go to step 6.

4. In the DATA LIST, check the output signal of the malfunctioning component.

- - If the output signal is OFF, go to step 5.

If the output signal is OFF, go to step 5.

- - If the output signal is not OFF, replace the control unit that controls the device that will not turn OFF.

If the output signal is not OFF, replace the control unit that controls the device that will not turn OFF.

5. Check the relay that does not stop or turn off, if applicable, then check for a short in the wire between the relay and the component, the relay and control unit, or the component and control unit.

- - If the relay and the wire harness are OK, replace the control unit that controls the component that will not stop or turn OFF.

If the relay and the wire harness are OK, replace the control unit that controls the component that will not stop or turn OFF.

- - If the relay and the wire harness are not OK, replace the relay or repair/replace the wire harness.

If the relay and the wire harness are not OK, replace the relay or repair/replace the wire harness.

6. Check the switch, then check for a short in the wire between the switch and the control unit that monitors the switch.

- - If the switch and the wire harness are OK, replace the control unit that monitors the switch.

If the switch and the wire harness are OK, replace the control unit that monitors the switch.

- - If the switch and the wire harness are not OK, replace the switch or repair/replace the appropriate wire harness.

If the switch and the wire harness are not OK, replace the switch or repair/replace the appropriate wire harness.
````

## Chunk 2197: Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode D - No DTCs and Output Won't Turn ON

- Title: Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode D - No DTCs and Output Won't Turn ON

# Body Electrical Troubleshooting - B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode D - No DTCs and Output Won't Turn ON

Do this diagnosis if a component that is controlled by the B-CAN system does not run or come on.

NOTE:

- If the component does not turn off or stop, go to B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode C .

- See the B-CAN system unit input/output index for a list of input and output devices and the control units that monitor the input and control the output devices.

1. Check the fuse of the malfunctioning output device.

- - If the fuse is OK, go to step 2.

If the fuse is OK, go to step 2.

- - If the fuse is not OK, replace the fuse. If the fuse blows again, repair the short to ground on the fuse circuit of the malfunctioning device.

If the fuse is not OK, replace the fuse. If the fuse blows again, repair the short to ground on the fuse circuit of the malfunctioning device.

2. Check for DTCs by selecting the system MODE MENU from the HDS.

- - If any DTCs are indicated, go to B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

If any DTCs are indicated, go to B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

- - If any DTCs are not indicated, go to step 3.

If any DTCs are not indicated, go to step 3.

3. Turn ON the switch that controls the malfunctioning component.

4. Select the applicable system from the system MODE MENU, then select DATA LIST, and check the input of the switch that controls the related component.

- - If there is an output signal, go to step 5.

If there is an output signal, go to step 5.

- - If there is not an output signal, go to step 9.

If there is not an output signal, go to step 9.

5. Check the relay (if applicable) and ground, then check for an open or a short in the circuit for the malfunctioning component.

- - If the relay and the wire harness are OK, go to step 6.

If the relay and the wire harness are OK, go to step 6.

- - If the relay and the wire harness are not OK, replace the relay or repair the wire circuit.

If the relay and the wire harness are not OK, replace the relay or repair the wire circuit.

6. Do the function test for the malfunctioning component.

- - If the output device pass the function test, go to step 7.

If the output device pass the function test, go to step 7.

- - If the output device does not pass the function test, replace the component.

If the output device does not pass the function test, replace the component.

7. With the malfunctioning output device connected, connect a voltmeter between the malfunctioning output device and body ground on the wire that the control unit uses to control the output device circuit.

8. Select FUNCTIONAL TESTS from the system MODE MENU, and do the forced operation test of the malfunctioning component.

- - If the voltage changes (12 V to 0 V, or 0 V to 12 V), inspect the ground for the component. If OK, replace the component.

If the voltage changes (12 V to 0 V, or 0 V to 12 V), inspect the ground for the component. If OK, replace the component.

- - If the voltage does not change, replace the control unit that controls the malfunctioning component.

If the voltage does not change, replace the control unit that controls the malfunctioning component.

9. Select DATA LIST from the system MODE MENU, and make sure the switch signal input for the malfunctioning system indicates a change when operated.

- - If the switch input indicates ON when the switch is ON, replace the control unit that controls the malfunctioning component.

If the switch input indicates ON when the switch is ON, replace the control unit that controls the malfunctioning component.

- - If the switch input does not indicate ON when the switch is ON, go to step 10.

If the switch input does not indicate ON when the switch is ON, go to step 10.

10. Check the switch and its ground (if applicable), then check for an open or a short in the wire between the switch and the control unit that monitors it.

- - If the switch and the wire harness are OK, replace the control unit that monitors the switch.

If the switch and the wire harness are OK, replace the control unit that monitors the switch.

- - If the switch and the wire harness are not OK, replace the switch, or repair/replace the wire harness.

If the switch and the wire harness are not OK, replace the switch, or repair/replace the wire harness.
````

## Chunk 2213: Driving Support DTC Troubleshooting Index (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

- Title: Driving Support DTC Troubleshooting Index (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

### Full Text

````text
# Driving Support DTC Troubleshooting Index (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

NOTE:

- The DTC troubleshooting index shows DTCs that is stored in the integrated driver support system. Each function of the integrated driver support system operates differently according to which DTC is stored. The functions of the integrated driver support system that operate in condition when these DTCs are stored can be confirmed with the table below: X: Indicator ON, and operation stop O: Operation stop Δ: Indicator blink, and operation stop

X: Indicator ON, and operation stop

O: Operation stop

Δ: Indicator blink, and operation stop

- For the multipurpose camera unit, different indicators may be lit for the same DTCs depending on which functions are in operation at the time of DTC stores. Make sure to check following DTCs with asterisks before Troubleshoot them.

NOTE: For details on the functions of indicator, and operation stop, refer to "How to Troubleshooting Information."

- How to Troubleshoot the Adaptive Cruise Control (ACC)

- How to Troubleshoot the Collision Mitigation Braking System (CMBS)

- How to Troubleshoot the Lane Keeping Assist System (LKAS)

- How to Troubleshoot the Road Departure Mitigation

- Brake System (How to Troubleshoot the VSA System)

- How to Troubleshoot the Auto High-Beam

DTC | Detection Item | Indicator Illumination/Operation in Case of a Failure

ACC System | CMBS | LKAS | RDM System | Brake System (Amber) | Auto High-Beam

B0050-F0 | Driver's Seat Belt Buckle Switch (Subfault) Fault Detection By SRS Unit | X | - | - | - | X | -

B2A00-92 | Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Detected Abnormal Signals) | X | X | - | X | X | -

B2A60-49 | Multipurpose Camera Unit Internal Electronic Failure | X | X | X | X | X | X

B2A60-54 | Static Camera Aiming Incomplete | Δ | Δ | Δ | Δ | - | o

B2A60-57 | Multipurpose Camera Unit Software Failure | X | X | X | X | X | X

B2A60-96 | Multipurpose Camera Unit Hardware Malfunction | X | X | X | X | X | X

B2A60-97 | Dust or Dirt On The Camera | - | X | o | X | - | o

B2A60-98 | Multipurpose Camera Unit Temperature Too High | - | X | o | X | - | o

B2A60-99 | Out of automatic correction range for Misalignment of Multipurpose Camera Unit image | o | X | X | X | - | X

B2A60-A2 | Power Supply Voltage Too Low (Multipurpose Camera Unit) | o | X | X | X | - | o

B2A60-A3 | Power Supply Voltage Too High (Multipurpose Camera Unit) | o | X | X | X | - | o

*1: When the system detects the ACC and the RDM failure while operating.

*2: When the system detects the CMBS failure while operating.

*3: When the millimeter wave radar receives gauge control module failure information.

*4: When related B-CAN system or switch failure information (judged by the gauge control module) is received.

*5: After the DTC is stored, the message (Radar Obstructed) is displayed on the MID at the initial stage in some models, and then the indicators come on.

DTC | Detection Item | Indicator Illumination/Operation in Case of a Failure

ACC System | CMBS | LKAS | RDM System | Brake System (Amber) | Auto High-Beam

B2A70-12 | LDW/RDM Switch Signal Circuit Short | - | - | - | X | X | -

B2A70-14 | LDW/RDM Switch Signal Circuit Short to Power | - | - | - | X | X | -

C0023-F0 | Brake Light Drive Relay Circuit Malfunction | X | X | - | X | X | -

C0031-62 | Left Front Wheel Speed Sensor Signal Error | X | X | X | X | X | -

C0034-62 | Right Front Wheel Speed Sensor Signal Error | X | X | X | X | X | -

C0037-62 | Left Rear Wheel Speed Sensor Signal Error | X | X | X | X | X | X

C003A-62 | Right Rear Wheel Speed Sensor Signal Error | X | X | X | X | X | X

C0041-14 | Brake Switch Signal Circuit Error | X | X | - | X | X | -

C0044-F0 | Master Cylinder Pressure Sensor Failure | X | X | o | X | X | -

C0051-54 | Steering Angle Neutral Position Learning Incomplete | X | X | o | X | - | o

C0051-96 | Steering Angle Sensor Malfunction | X | X | o | X | X | o

C0061-9A | Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Unstable Lateral Acceleration Sensor Signal) | - | - | - | - | - | o

C0061-F0 | Lateral Acceleration Sensor(Subfault) Fault Detection By Source Module | - | - | - | - | - | o

C0062-9A | Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Unstable Longitudinal Acceleration Sensor Signal) | o | X | - | X | - | -

C0062-F0 | Longitudinal Acceleration Sensor (Subfault) Fault Detection By Source Module | X | X | - | X | X | -

## Chunk 2217: CMBS Indicator (Amber)

- Title: CMBS Indicator (Amber)
- Source path: `pages\1083.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_eeb89cdc8b78`
- Images: `images\GHH401519.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\16669.html`

### Full Text

````text
# CMBS Indicator (Amber)

When the CMBS detects a problem during operation, the CMBS stops operating, and the CMBS indicator and the brake system indicator (amber) turns on.

NOTE:

- For CMBS function, review the Collision Mitigation Braking System (CMBS) Description .

- The CMBS communicates with other control units via the F-CAN. For CMBS diagram, refer to the System Diagram for CMBS . If a connected unit malfunctions, the CMBS will not operate. In this case, both CMBS indicator and connected unit indicator come on and stay on (except for the gauge control module).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

# Self-Diagnostic Function

When a problem is detected by the self-diagnostic function, the CMBS stops operating, turns on the CMBS indicator, indicates the problem on the multi-information display (MID), and stores a DTC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

## Chunk 2220: CMBS Indication

- Title: CMBS Indication
- Source path: `pages\1086.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_3e9997a14703`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\16672.html`

### Full Text

````text
# CMBS Indication

- When the CMBS is OK, the CMBS indicator comes on for a few seconds, then goes off after turning the vehicle to the ON mode.

- When you set the CMBS to OFF, the multipurpose camera unit turns on the CMBS indicator.

- When the system is activated, the MID indicates a brake system warning indicator and a warning buzzer sounds.

- If the system stops operating because of a problem, the MID indicates the problem.
````

## Chunk 2221: Millimeter Wave Radar

- Title: Millimeter Wave Radar
- Source path: `pages\1087.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_c8b4418f3efa`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\16673.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Millimeter Wave Radar

The millimeter wave radar measures the distance between your vehicle and the vehicle directly ahead to calculate the difference in speed between the vehicles.

2/4-door: The millimeter wave radar also has an error detection function which sends a signal to the multipurpose camera unit if an error is detected. The multipurpose camera unit receives this information from the millimeter wave radar.

5-door: The millimeter wave radar has a self-diagnostic function. If the system detects a problem, it disables the CMBS, and turns on the CMBS indicator.
````

## Chunk 2222: Dirt or dust detective function (Clean Millimeter Wave Radar Indication)

- Title: Dirt or dust detective function (Clean Millimeter Wave Radar Indication)
- Source path: `pages\1088.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_1a5b5224164d`
- Images: `images\GHH401521.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\16674.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Dirt or dust detective function (Clean Millimeter Wave Radar Indication)

If the millimeter wave radar or radar cover gets dirty, the CMBS stops operating and the following message will appear: Some Driver Assist Systems Cannot Operate: Radar Obstructed appears on the MID. Clean the millimeter wave radar and the radar cover surface. Once the millimeter wave radar and radar cover surface are clean, the message on the MID disappears and the CMBS automatically turns on.

NOTE:

- Under the above conditions DTC P2583-97 is stored.

- When it is snowing, the radio waves transmitted and received by the millimeter wave radar may be refracted by the snow or ice attached around the millimeter wave radar such as the front grille. At this time, DTC P2583-76 is stored depending on the situation.

- After DTC P2583-76 is stored, the message (Radar Obstructed) is displayed on the MID at the initial stage in some models, and then the indicators come on.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

## Chunk 2224: Multipurpose Camera Unit

````text
# Multipurpose Camera Unit

The multipurpose camera unit controls the brakes and the various driver warnings when the system recognizes the possibility of a collision.

The multipurpose camera unit also has an error detection function which sends a signal to the millimeter wave radar if an error is detected. The millimeter wave radar receives this information from the multipurpose camera unit.
````

## Chunk 2225: Dirt or dust detective function (CLEAN FRONT WINDSHIELD Indication)

- Title: Dirt or dust detective function (CLEAN FRONT WINDSHIELD Indication)
- Source path: `pages\1091.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_08e6c0d27c10`
- Images: `images\GHH401522.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\16677.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Dirt or dust detective function (CLEAN FRONT WINDSHIELD Indication)

If the following message appears: Some Driver Assist System Cannot Operate: Clean Front Windshield appears on the MID, the camera lens or the windshield has enough dirt so that the camera view is obstructed, the CMBS stops operating.

Clean any dirt or contamination on the camera lens or the windshield. Once the camera's view is unobstructed, the MID turns off and the CMBS automatically turns back to ON.

NOTE: When millimeter wave radar detected dirt or dust on the millimeter wave radar and radar cover surface, DTC P2583-97 is stored as a past failure.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

## Chunk 2226: System Protect Function (High temperature) (MULTIPURPOSE CAMERA HOT Indication)

### Full Text

````text
# System Protect Function (High temperature) (MULTIPURPOSE CAMERA HOT Indication)

2/4-door: If the following message appears: Some Driver Assist Systems Cannot Operate: Camera Temperature Too High appears on the MID, the CMBS stops operating

5-door: If the following message appears: Some Driver Assist Systems Cannot Operate: Camera Temperature Too High appears on the MID, the CMBS stops operating, and stores a DTC.

When the multipurpose camera temperature cools down to its proper operating temperature, the indicator on the MID turns off, and the CMBS automatically resumes operation.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

- Duplicate sources: `pages\16680.html`

### Full Text

````text
# CAN Communication

The CMBS communicates with other control units via the F-CAN. For CMBS diagram, refer to the System Diagram for CMBS .

If the millimeter wave radar and the multipurpose camera unit detects a communication error or a malfunction in another connected unit, the CMBS system will not operate even though it is OK. In this case, both the CMBS indicator and the related unit indicator will come on and stay on.
````

## Chunk 2229: How to Troubleshoot DTCs

- Title: How to Troubleshoot DTCs
- Source path: `pages\1095.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_8a3d40032a6c`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\16681.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Troubleshoot DTCs

Check the DTCs with the HDS.

NOTE: For CMBS DTCs, refer to the DTC troubleshooting index .

Before troubleshooting, check and note these items:

- Ask the customer about the conditions when the problem occurred, and try to reproduce the same conditions for troubleshooting.

- If the symptom does not appear and the CMBS indicator does not come on while test-driving, but troubleshooting is done based on the DTC, check for poor connections or loose terminals at all connectors related to the circuit that you are troubleshooting.

- After the troubleshooting or repairs are done, clear the DTCs, and test-drive the vehicle under the same conditions as when the DTC was originally set. Make sure the CMBS indicator does not come on.

- The CMBS may sometimes stop control because of the environment (weather, road conditions, driving conditions, etc.). A intermittent failure DTCs.
````

## Chunk 2230: How to Retrieve DTCs

- Title: How to Retrieve DTCs
- Source path: `pages\1097.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_96339f5425f3`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1189.html`, `pages\16683.html`, `pages\16588.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Retrieve DTCs

NOTE: Make sure the 12 volt battery is fully charged before you begin.

1. Connect the HDS

2. Use the HDS to check for Integrated Driver Support System for DTCs.

3. Read and record the DTC.

4. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

5. Disconnect the HDS.

6. Do the troubleshooting procedure for the DTC.
````

## Chunk 2231: How to Clear DTCs

- Title: How to Clear DTCs
- Source path: `pages\1098.html`

- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\1190.html`, `pages\16684.html`, `pages\16589.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Clear DTCs

NOTE: Make sure the 12 volt battery is fully charged before you begin.

1. Connect the HDS

2. In the DRIVING SUPPORT of the HDS, select Integrated Driver Support System, then DTCs choose the clear DTCs icon, and follow the prompts.

3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

4. Disconnect the HDS.
````

## Chunk 2232: LKAS Indicator (Amber)

- Duplicate sources: `pages\16685.html`

### Full Text

````text
# LKAS Indicator (Amber)

When the LKAS detects a problem while operating, the LKAS stops operating, and the LKAS indicator stays on.

NOTE: The LKAS communicates with the EPS motor/control unit, the gauge control module, the PCM, the VSA modulator-control unit, and the SRS unit via the F-CAN. If a connected unit malfunctions, the LKAS will not operate. In this case, both the LKAS indicator and the connected unit indicator come on and stay on (except for the gauge control module).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

## Chunk 2238: How to Troubleshoot DTCs

- Title: How to Troubleshoot DTCs
- Source path: `pages\1105.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_c35ccd7be779`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\16691.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Troubleshoot DTCs

Check for DTCs with the HDS. Make a copy of the freeze data, then clear the DTC.

Before troubleshooting, check and note these items:

- Ask the customer about the conditions when the problem occurred, and try to reproduce the same conditions for troubleshooting.

- If the symptom does not appear and the LKAS indicator does not come on while test-driving and if your troubleshooting was based on a DTC, check for poor connections or loose terminals at all connectors related to the circuit that you are troubleshooting.

- After the troubleshooting or repairs are done, clear the DTCs, and test-drive the vehicle under the same conditions as when the DTC was originally set. Make sure the LKAS indicator does not come on.

- The LKAS sometimes stops operating because of the environment (weather, road conditions, driving conditions, etc.). A DTC may be stored in these cases.
````

## Chunk 2239: Intermittent Failures

### Full Text

````text
# Intermittent Failures

The term "intermittent failure" means a system may have had a failure, but it checks OK now. If you cannot reproduce the condition, check for loose connections and terminals. Also check ground and power connections related to the circuit that you are troubleshooting.
````

## Chunk 2240: How to Retrieve DTCs

- Title: How to Retrieve DTCs
- Source path: `pages\1107.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_aeb6f53a9545`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\16693.html`

### Full Text

````text
# How to Retrieve DTCs

NOTE: Make sure the 12 volt battery is fully charged before you begin.

1. Connect the HDS .

2. Use the HDS to check for Integrated Driver Support System for DTCs.

3. Read and record the DTC:

- Freeze data indicates the LKAS conditions that were detected when the first system malfunction activated the indicator.

- The HDS can read the DTC, the freeze data, the current data, and other system data.

4. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

5. Disconnect the HDS.

6. Do the troubleshooting procedure for the DTC.
````

# DTC B2A00-92 (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC B2A00-92 : Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Detected Abnormal Signals)

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- This DTC is recorded when the millimeter wave radar and multipurpose camera receive an unexpected or out of range yaw rate, steering angle, or wheel speed sensor signal via F-CAN.

- The specific driving conditions that may cause this DTC to be recorded are as follows:

- - The vehicle is being driven in a manner that exceeds the capabilities and control of the driving support system. Question the customer about how and where the vehicle was being driven at the time of the warning. - Sharp turn, sudden steering, rapid acceleration or deceleration. - When the vehicle is driven in mountains or rough roads. - When the vehicle loses traction while driving (wheel spin or lock up).

- - The vehicle is being driven in a manner that exceeds the capabilities and control of the driving support system. Question the customer about how and where the vehicle was being driven at the time of the warning.

The vehicle is being driven in a manner that exceeds the capabilities and control of the driving support system. Question the customer about how and where the vehicle was being driven at the time of the warning.

- - Sharp turn, sudden steering, rapid acceleration or deceleration.

Sharp turn, sudden steering, rapid acceleration or deceleration.

- - When the vehicle is driven in mountains or rough roads.

When the vehicle is driven in mountains or rough roads.

- - When the vehicle loses traction while driving (wheel spin or lock up).

When the vehicle loses traction while driving (wheel spin or lock up).

- If input data from another system on F-CAN is out of range or incorrect. Inspect these systems for DTCs and/or OBS data and troubleshoot as needed.

- If this DTC is recorded, the integrated driver support system temporarily cancels its control.

- If this DTC is recorded, turn the vehicle OFF mode and ON mode again on the flat place or then test-drive for 10 minutes stably at 25 mph (40 km/h) or more to restore the vehicle to normal.

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

DTC Description | DTC

B2A00-92 Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Detected Abnormal Signals)

DTC (IDAS)

- DTC check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then wait for at least 6 seconds. -2. Check for DTCs with the HDS. YES Perform a troubleshooting for the recorded DTC. NO Clear the DTC with the HDS and explain to the customer that it is not a failure.

# DTC B2A60-98, P2583-98 (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC B2A60-98 : Multipurpose Camera Unit Temperature Too High

DTC P2583-98 : Millimeter Wave Radar Temperature Too High

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- These DTC are recorded when the vehicle is left in the scorching heat, is in a traffic jam at high temperature, or is driving uphill continuously and the millimeter wave radar and the multipurpose camera unit gets extremely hot.

- The function automatically returns to normal when the internal temperatures of the millimeter wave radar and the multipurpose camera unit drop.

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

DTC Description | DTC

B2A60-98 Multipurpose Camera Unit Temperature Too High

P2583-98 Millimeter Wave Radar Temperature Too High

DTC (IDAS)

- Inspecting the temperature in the control unit -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Check the parameter(s) of the internal temperatures of the millimeter wave radar and the multipurpose camera with the HDS. Is the temperature 185 deg.F (85 deg.C) or less? YES Go to step 2. NO Cool the control unit until the internal temperature is 167 deg.F (75 deg.C) or less. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Check the parameter(s) of the internal temperatures of the millimeter wave radar and the multipurpose camera with the HDS.

Is the temperature 185 deg.F (85 deg.C) or less?

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

Cool the control unit until the internal temperature is 167 deg.F (75 deg.C) or less. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

- Problem verification: NOTE: If the control unit has an internal temperature of 167 deg.F (75 deg.C) or more, cool the control unit until the internal temperature is 167 deg.F (75 deg.C) or less. -1. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -2. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then wait for at least 6 seconds. -4. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC B2A60-98 Multipurpose Camera Unit Temperature Too High P2583-98 Millimeter Wave Radar Temperature Too High Is DTC B2A60-98 or P2583-98 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Faulty a control unit, replace the control unit related to the DTC. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. The control unit was temporarily in a high temperature state.

NOTE: If the control unit has an internal temperature of 167 deg.F (75 deg.C) or more, cool the control unit until the internal temperature is 167 deg.F (75 deg.C) or less.

-1. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-2. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then wait for at least 6 seconds.

-4. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC

B2A60-98 Multipurpose Camera Unit Temperature Too High

P2583-98 Millimeter Wave Radar Temperature Too High

Is DTC B2A60-98 or P2583-98 indicated?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Faulty a control unit, replace the control unit related to the DTC.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. The control unit was temporarily in a high temperature state.

# DTC B2A60-99 (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC B2A60-99 : Out of automatic correction range for Misalignment of Multipurpose Camera Unit image

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If this DTC is recorded, the following is suspected:

- - Improper installation of the multipurpose camera unit (aging of vehicle, incorrect assembly, etc.). - The multipurpose camera unit installing portion is deformed by a minor collision or strong impact. - The axis of the multipurpose camera unit is out of alignment due to abnormal wheel alignment, abnormal tire pressure, etc. - Altered suspension

- - Improper installation of the multipurpose camera unit (aging of vehicle, incorrect assembly, etc.).

Improper installation of the multipurpose camera unit (aging of vehicle, incorrect assembly, etc.).

- - The multipurpose camera unit installing portion is deformed by a minor collision or strong impact.

The multipurpose camera unit installing portion is deformed by a minor collision or strong impact.

- - The axis of the multipurpose camera unit is out of alignment due to abnormal wheel alignment, abnormal tire pressure, etc.

The axis of the multipurpose camera unit is out of alignment due to abnormal wheel alignment, abnormal tire pressure, etc.

- - Altered suspension

Altered suspension

- After inspecting, repairing, or adjusting a possible cause of the failure, be sure to perform multipurpose camera unit aiming . If the DTC cannot be deleted after multipurpose camera unit aiming, replace the multipurpose camera unit .

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

DTC U3006-16 : Power Supply Voltage Too Low (Millimeter Wave Radar)

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Before performing a troubleshooting, check the 12 volt battery voltage. If the voltage is less than 12 volt, charge the battery.

# DTC C0061-9A, C0062-9A, C0063-9A (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC C0061-9A : Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Unstable Lateral Acceleration Sensor Signal)

DTC C0062-9A : Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Unstable Longitudinal Acceleration Sensor Signal)

DTC C0063-9A : Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Unstable Yaw Rate Sensor Signal)

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- These DTCs are recorded when the sensor signal reliability temporarily decreases due to a drop in the power supply voltage supplied to the yaw rate sensor and lateral acceleration sensor built in the SRS unit or for other reason.

- If this DTC is recorded, some of the integrated driver support system functions are temporarily canceled but it is not a failure.

- When the power supply voltage supplied to the yaw rate sensor and lateral acceleration sensor built in the SRS unit return to normal, the temporarily cancelled integrated driver support system functions are re-activated.

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

DTC Description | DTC

C0061-9A Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Unstable Lateral Acceleration Sensor Signal)

C0062-9A Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Unstable Longitudinal Acceleration Sensor Signal)

C0063-9A Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Unstable Yaw Rate Sensor Signal)

DTC (IDAS)

- DTC check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then wait for at least 6 seconds. -2. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC C0061-9A Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Unstable Lateral Acceleration Sensor Signal) DTC Description DTC C0062-9A Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Unstable Longitudinal Acceleration Sensor Signal) C0063-9A Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Unstable Yaw Rate Sensor Signal) Is there any DTC(s) other than DTC C0061-9A, C0062-9A, or C0063-9A? YES Perform a troubleshooting for the recorded DTC. NO Clear the DTC with the HDS and explain to the customer that it is not a failure.

# DTC C0063-F0 (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC C0063-F0 : Yaw Rate Sensor (Subfault) Fault Detection By Source Module

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- This vehicle's millimeter wave radar receives the failure information of the yaw rate sensor from the SRS unit. The SRS unit does not uses the yaw rate sensor for airbag deployment control, so the SRS system indicator does not come on even if the yaw rate sensor is faulty. If this DTC is stored, clear the DTC and then perform problem verification according to the troubleshooting flow. If this DTC is stored again, replace the SRS unit.

- If the DTC is recorded after it is cleared with the HDS, replace the SRS unit .

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

# DTC C1040-00 (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC C1040-00 : Wheel Speed Signal Error

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- This DTC is recorded when the estimated yaw rate calculated based on the wheel speeds of the right and left wheels differs between the front and rear wheels.

- The specific conditions under which this DTC are as follows:

- - Abnormal tire diameter (low tire pressure, uneven wear, etc.) - Driving on a road having different road conditions on the right and left sides

- - Abnormal tire diameter (low tire pressure, uneven wear, etc.)

Abnormal tire diameter (low tire pressure, uneven wear, etc.)

- - Driving on a road having different road conditions on the right and left sides

Driving on a road having different road conditions on the right and left sides

- If this DTC is recorded, the integrated driver support system temporarily cancels its control to prevent the system from selecting an incorrect leading vehicle.

- When the difference in the estimated yaw rate between the front and rear wheels returns to normal, the integrated driver support system resumes its control.

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

DTC Description | DTC

C1040-00 Wheel Speed Signal Error

DTC (IDAS)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK). -4. Test-drive the vehicle on a straight road at speeds 25 mph (40 km/h) or more for 10 minutes or more. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC C1040-00 Wheel Speed Signal Error Is DTC C1040-00 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Check the tire pressure and inspect for uneven wear. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK).

-4. Test-drive the vehicle on a straight road at speeds 25 mph (40 km/h) or more for 10 minutes or more.

-5. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

DTC C1C00-F0 : APP Sensor Signal Failure

DTC U0401-68 : PCM (PGM-FI System) Malfunction

DTC U0402-68 : PCM/TCM Malfunction

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- This DTC is recorded when the millimeter wave radar receives failure information for the PCM via the F-CAN.

- If a DTC is recorded again after it is cleared with the HDS, perform a troubleshooting for the PGM-FI system .

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

# DTC P0833-14 (M/T) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC P0833-14 : Clutch ON/OFF Signal Circuit Error

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If a DTC related to the electric parking brake system is recorded at the same time, first perform a troubleshooting for the electric parking brake system .

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

DTC Description | DTC

P0833-14 Clutch ON/OFF Signal Circuit Error

DTC (IDAS)

- Clutch switch operation check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Check the parameter(s) of the clutch switch change when the clutch pedal is operated with the HDS. Do the parameter(s) change when the clutch pedal is operated? YES Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Clear the DTC with the HDS. NO Go to the indicated DTC's troubleshooting in the electric parking brake system .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Check the parameter(s) of the clutch switch change when the clutch pedal is operated with the HDS.

Do the parameter(s) change when the clutch pedal is operated?

YES

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

NO

Go to the indicated DTC's troubleshooting in the electric parking brake system .
````

# DTC P2583-49, U3000-49, U3000-57 (Millimeter Wave Radar) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC P2583-49 : Millimeter Wave Radar Internal Electronic Failure

DTC U3000-49 : Millimeter Wave Radar Internal Electronic Failure

DTC U3000-57 : Millimeter Wave Radar Software Failure

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

DTC Description | DTC

P2583-49 Millimeter Wave Radar Internal Electronic Failure

U3000-49 Millimeter Wave Radar Internal Electronic Failure

U3000-57 Millimeter Wave Radar Software Failure

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to OFF (LOCK) mode and then ON mode repeatedly at least 3 times. -4. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC P2583-49 Millimeter Wave Radar Internal Electronic Failure U3000-49 Millimeter Wave Radar Internal Electronic Failure U3000-57 Millimeter Wave Radar Software Failure Is DTC P2583-49, U3000-49, or U3000-57 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Replace the millimeter wave radar . NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting .

### Full Text

````text
# DTC P2583-76 (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC P2583-76 : Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Misalignment Millimeter Wave Radar)

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Before you troubleshoot, make sure all the tire pressures are correct, and remove all the cargo from the vehicle.

- This DTC is recorded if the beam axis of the millimeter wave radar gets out of alignment as follows while the vehicle is driving.

- - Vertical direction: 3.0 degrees or more - Horizontal direction: 2.5 degrees or more

- - Vertical direction: 3.0 degrees or more

Vertical direction: 3.0 degrees or more

- - Horizontal direction: 2.5 degrees or more

Horizontal direction: 2.5 degrees or more

- If this DTC is recorded, the following is suspected:

- - Change in the vehicle posture caused by an overload or the vehicle being towed. - It has been detected that the beam axis of the millimeter wave radar is out of alignment due to improper installation. - The millimeter wave radar installing portion is deformed by a minor collision or strong impact. - The beam axis of the millimeter wave radar is out of alignment due to abnormal wheel alignment, abnormal tire pressure, etc. - Altered suspension - When it is snowing, the radio waves transmitted and received by the millimeter wave radar may be refracted by the snow or ice attached around the millimeter wave radar such as the front grille, and are detected as a misalignment.

- - Change in the vehicle posture caused by an overload or the vehicle being towed.

Change in the vehicle posture caused by an overload or the vehicle being towed.

- - It has been detected that the beam axis of the millimeter wave radar is out of alignment due to improper installation.

It has been detected that the beam axis of the millimeter wave radar is out of alignment due to improper installation.

Altered suspension

- - When it is snowing, the radio waves transmitted and received by the millimeter wave radar may be refracted by the snow or ice attached around the millimeter wave radar such as the front grille, and are detected as a misalignment.

When it is snowing, the radio waves transmitted and received by the millimeter wave radar may be refracted by the snow or ice attached around the millimeter wave radar such as the front grille, and are detected as a misalignment.

- If this DTC is recorded, the integrated drive support system temporarily cancels its control to prevent excessive operation or malfunction.

- Even when this DTC is recorded, the vehicle returns to normal if the misalignment of the detection axis of the millimeter wave radar is 1.5 degrees or less.

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

DTC Description | DTC

- Check the vehicle condition -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Check if any integrated driver support system related indicator comes on in the gauge control module. Is any indicator currently comes on? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO The system is OK at this time. Clear the DTC with the HDS. If the indicator is turned off, and axis misalignment has been recorded, explain to the customer that the system detected a temporarily misaligned millimeter wave axis and stopped functioning.

# DTC P2583-92 (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC P2583-92 : CMBS or FCW Over Working

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- This DTC is recorded when the CMBS is activated several times in a single vehicle ON mode cycle.

- If this DTC is recorded, the CMBS function does not return to normal until the DTC is cleared with the HDS.

- If this DTC is recorded, the following is suspected:

- - The millimeter wave radar is installed improperly and close-range obstacles are detected frequently. - The millimeter wave radar installing portion is deformed by a minor collision or strong impact. - The beam axis of the millimeter wave radar is out of alignment due to abnormal wheel alignment, abnormal tire pressure, etc. - The CMBS is activated several times in a row intentionally by the driver.

- - The millimeter wave radar is installed improperly and close-range obstacles are detected frequently.

The millimeter wave radar is installed improperly and close-range obstacles are detected frequently.

- - The millimeter wave radar installing portion is deformed by a minor collision or strong impact.

The millimeter wave radar installing portion is deformed by a minor collision or strong impact.

- - The beam axis of the millimeter wave radar is out of alignment due to abnormal wheel alignment, abnormal tire pressure, etc.

The beam axis of the millimeter wave radar is out of alignment due to abnormal wheel alignment, abnormal tire pressure, etc.

- - The CMBS is activated several times in a row intentionally by the driver.

The CMBS is activated several times in a row intentionally by the driver.

- After inspecting, repairing, or adjusting a possible cause of the failure, be sure to perform millimeter wave radar aiming . If the DTC cannot be deleted after millimeter wave radar aiming, replace the millimeter wave radar .

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

DTC Description | DTC

P2583-92 CMBS or FCW Over Working

DTC (IDAS)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then wait for at least 6 seconds. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC P2583-92 CMBS or FCW Over Working Is DTC P2583-92 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting .

# DTC P2583-97 (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC P2583-97 : Dust or Dirt On The Millimeter Wave Radar

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- This DTC is stored when the millimeter wave radar determines that the detection capability (sensitivity) has decreased. A decrease in the detection capability (sensitivity) of the millimeter wave radar does not always mean a there is an obstruction on the radar surface.

- - Wipe off any dirt or dust off the millimeter wave radar surface and cover and test drive the vehicle. If the DTC is not record again, the millimeter wave radar is OK.

- - Wipe off any dirt or dust off the millimeter wave radar surface and cover and test drive the vehicle. If the DTC is not record again, the millimeter wave radar is OK.

Wipe off any dirt or dust off the millimeter wave radar surface and cover and test drive the vehicle. If the DTC is not record again, the millimeter wave radar is OK.

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

DTC Description | DTC

P2583-97 Dust or Dirt On The Millimeter Wave Radar

DTC (IDAS)

- Millimeter wave radar and radar cover visual check -1. Check for dirt and dust on the radar cover surface. -2. Remove the radar cover, and check for dirt and dust on the inner side of the radar cover. -3. Check for dirt and dust on the millimeter wave radar surface. Is it surface dirty? YES Clean the millimeter wave radar and/or the radar cover. After cleaning, install the radar cover, then go to step 2. NO Go to step 2.

-1. Check for dirt and dust on the radar cover surface.

-2. Remove the radar cover, and check for dirt and dust on the inner side of the radar cover.

-3. Check for dirt and dust on the millimeter wave radar surface.

Is it surface dirty?

YES

Clean the millimeter wave radar and/or the radar cover. After cleaning, install the radar cover, then go to step 2.

NO

Go to step 2.

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Test-drive the vehicle at speeds 25 mph (40 km/h) or more for 2 minutes. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. # DTC P2583-9A (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC P2583-9A : Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Jammed Millimeter Wave Radar)

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- This DTC is recorded when the millimeter wave radar malfunctions due to the impact of external radio waves.

- If this DTC is recorded, the integrated driver support system temporarily cancels its control.

- If this DTC is recorded, the vehicle returns to normal if the factor causing the failure has disappeared when the vehicle is stopped (vehicle OFF mode) and is turned to the ON mode again.

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

DTC Description | DTC

P2583-9A Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Jammed Millimeter Wave Radar)

DTC (IDAS)

- DTC check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then wait for at least 6 seconds. -2. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC P2583-9A Temporary stop of Integrated Driver Support System (Jammed Millimeter Wave Radar) Is there any DTC(s) other than DTC P2583-9A? YES Perform a troubleshooting for the recorded DTC. NO Clear the DTC with the HDS and explain to the customer that it is not a failure.

- Before you troubleshoot, review the how to troubleshooting information .

- This DTC is stored by communication failure between the F-CAN line and the millimeter wave radar. When DTC U0029-00 is indicated, the F-CAN line has already returned to normal. # DTC U0100-00, U0101-00, U0122-00, U0128-00, U0131-00, U0151-00, U0155-00 (2/4-door: Millimeter Wave Radar) (2019 2020 2021)

DTC U0100-00 : Lost Communication With The PCM (PGM-FI System)

DTC U0101-00 : Lost Communication With The PCM/TCM

DTC U0122-00 : Lost Communication With The VSA Modulator-Control Unit

DTC U0128-00 : Lost Communication With The Electric Parking Brake Control Unit

DTC U0131-00 : Lost Communication With The EPS Control Unit

DTC U0151-00 : Lost Communication With The SRS Unit

DTC U0155-00 : Lost Communication With The Gauge Control Module

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If the transmitting control unit is malfunctioning, a DTC may be recorded. According to the recorded DTC, inspect the power circuits and ground circuits of the units with which the millimeter wave radar does not communicate.

- The functions of the integrated driver support system that will be temporarily canceled when this DTC is recorded can be confirmed with the DTC troubleshooting index .

U0151-00 Lost Communication With The SRS Unit

U0155-00 Lost Communication With The Gauge Control Module

U0101-00 | Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the CAN gateway and the PCM. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting, or substitute a good-known PCM , then recheck. If they are OK, replace the original PCM .

DTC | Operation for transmitting control unit

U0131-00 | Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the CAN gateway and the EPS control unit. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting. If they are OK, replace the EPS motor/control unit .

U0151-00 | Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the CAN gateway and the SRS unit. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting. If they are OK, replace the SRS unit .

NO

Repair an open in the F-CAN B_H wire or the F-CAN B_L wire between the transmitting control unit and the CAN gateway.
